    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

AI

JAH 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator

Sources

JAH Network live manifest (63,047 databases / 12,349,330 records); JAH Network AI Access guide (docs/AI_ACCESS.md); JAH Network Master List (ChatGPT catalog, 2026-11-12)

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator content-hash dedupe — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

Method

Hash the record's canonical JSON (sorted keys), look the hash up in the archive index, and refuse duplicates with the existing record's ID cited. The hash covers content only, never timestamps.

Dataset

The live database this pair companions, read from the local clone and the live manifest.

Verification path

python3 -c "import gzip,json,subprocess,sys; rows=[json.loads(l) for l in gzip.open('data/15-minute-cities-python-generator/index.jsonl.gz','rt')]; r=[x for x in rows if x['id']=='JAH-15PY-000029'][0]; open('/tmp/_gen.py','w').write(r['demo']['code']); print(subprocess.run([sys.executable,'/tmp/_gen.py'],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout==r['expected_output'])"

Formal equation

score = (covered/total + passed/checked) / 2 x 100

Formal equation, in plain words

covered: items this generator covers; total: items in scope; passed: checks passed; checked: checks run; score: mean of coverage and pass rate, in percent.

Formal status

machine-checked by demo (generator executed twice, outputs identical)

Scientific equation

score = (covered/total + passed/checked) / 2 x 100

Proof sketch

The demo runs generate(seed) twice in one process, asserts byte-identity, validates the generated record, and prints both digests; re-running must reproduce expected_output byte-for-byte.

Proof status

machine-checked by demo (generator execution + validation)

Results reported

The template self-test proves the hash is stable across runs and changes when the content changes; the CLI refuses duplicate hashes.

Skeptical analysis

The generator produces records only for its own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table.

Specification

  • care: Re-run the generator before citing its output; the seed is part of the record's identity.
  • dimensions: {'checked': 8, 'covered': 63048, 'passed': 8, 'total': 63048}
  • materials: stdlib-only Python 3

Boundless scales

  • A1: Single-database scale: this generator on one database.
  • A2: Network scale: the companion pattern across all 63,047 databases.
  • A3: Boundless scale: the generator across every generated scale of every file.

Key figures

  • checked: 8
  • covered: 63048
  • passed: 8
  • total: 63048

Working demo program

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Content-hash dedupe'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Content-hash dedupe'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Input schema JAH-15PY-000030 · complex

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

The input schema states exactly what the generator accepts — seed (integer), topic (title from the topic table), count (1-100) — so a caller can build a valid call without reading the source.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

The input schema states exactly what the generator accepts — seed (integer), topic (title from the topic table), count (1-100) — so a caller can build a valid call without reading the source.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator input schema — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Declare the inputs in the CLI help and in the companion-record schema; validate every call against them before running. A call that fails validation is refused with the violation named.

The Answer

The template CLI enforces the throttle (count above 100 is refused with exit code 2) and the schema file validates the record shape.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Input schema'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):

    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)

        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Output schema'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Output schema'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems

        second = self.generate(seed)

        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)

    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator output schema — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

Method

Emit one JSON object per record with the fixed keys, sorted for stability; the schema file pins the keys and their types. A record missing a key fails validation.

Dataset

The live database this pair companions, read from the local clone and the live manifest.

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Output schema'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Error codes JAH-15PY-000032 · compounded

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Error codes give every generator failure a stable exit status — 2 for a bad call, 3 for a validation failure, 4 for a duplicate — so a caller can handle failures instead of guessing.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Error codes give every generator failure a stable exit status — 2 for a bad call, 3 for a validation failure, 4 for a duplicate — so a caller can handle failures instead of guessing.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator error codes — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Return the code with a human sentence on stderr; never return a bare traceback to a caller. Codes are drawn from the template's list, never invented per pilot.

The Answer

Error codes are implemented in the template CLI (exit 2/3/4) and inherited by every pilot generator.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

Batching and throttle JAH-15PY-000033 · complex

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Batching caps one call at 100 records (throttle_per_call) so a runaway call cannot flood the archive; larger jobs are split into batches of 100.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Batching caps one call at 100 records (throttle_per_call) so a runaway call cannot flood the archive; larger jobs are split into batches of 100.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator batching and throttle — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Enforce the throttle in the runner: any count above 100 is refused before generation starts. The limit is recorded in every record's generator field.

The Answer

The throttle is proven by the template CLI (count 101 is refused) and recorded in all 288 pilot records.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 1000000000, 'covered': 100, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Batching and throttle'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 1000000000, 'covered': 100, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Batching and throttle'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Generator

Python generator, prefix JAH-15PY-GEN-, seed-derived IDs, deterministic, validated, content-hash deduped. Ceiling 1,000,000,000 scales per file, generated on demand by the file AI — not stored files.

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 1000000000, 'covered': 100, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Batching and throttle'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Versioning JAH-15PY-000034 · simple

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Versioning stamps the pattern version (v1.0.0, 2026-11-12) into the generator, so a record can always be traced to the exact pattern version that made it.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Versioning stamps the pattern version (v1.0.0, 2026-11-12) into the generator, so a record can always be traced to the exact pattern version that made it.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator versioning — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Record the pattern version in PATTERN.md and in each pilot's manifest entry; when the pattern changes, the version changes and old generators stay runnable so old records stay regenerable.

The Answer

Pattern v1.0.0 is the version stamped on all three pilots; no earlier version exists.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Generator chaining JAH-15PY-000035 · compounded

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Chaining feeds one generator's output into the next generator's input — a coverage generator feeding a validation generator — so a pipeline can run as one job.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Chaining feeds one generator's output into the next generator's input — a coverage generator feeding a validation generator — so a pipeline can run as one job.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator generator chaining — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Declare each link's output schema and the next generator's input schema, and check they match before running. A link whose schemas mismatch is refused before any generation happens.

The Answer

Chaining is a design in this record: the pilots' generators are standalone, and no chained run is claimed.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 0, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Generator chaining'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 0, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Generator chaining'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Generator

Python generator, prefix JAH-15PY-GEN-, seed-derived IDs, deterministic, validated, content-hash deduped. Ceiling 1,000,000,000 scales per file, generated on demand by the file AI — not stored files.

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 0, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Generator chaining'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Output archiving JAH-15PY-000036 · complex

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Output archiving stores accepted records with their seed, generator version, and content hash, so any archived record can be verified or regenerated later.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Output archiving stores accepted records with their seed, generator version, and content hash, so any archived record can be verified or regenerated later.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator output archiving — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Write each accepted record to the companion database's index with its seed and hash; an archived record is never edited in place — a correction is a new record that cites the old one.

The Answer

Archiving is the companion database itself: 288 pilot records stored as gz indexes with their seeds and hashes.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 288, 'covered': 288, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Output archiving'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Command-line interface JAH-15PY-000037 · simple

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

The CLI is how a human runs the generator: --seed, --topic, --count, --self-test, with exit codes that say what happened.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

The CLI is how a human runs the generator: --seed, --topic, --count, --self-test, with exit codes that say what happened.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator command-line interface — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Parse arguments with argparse, run the requested generation or self-test, and print one JSON record per line to stdout. Errors go to stderr with an exit code, never to stdout mixed with records.

The Answer

The template CLI is proven by execution (--self-test 8/8, --seed 7 --count 2 prints two valid records).

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Command-line interface'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Command-line interface'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Generator

Python generator, prefix JAH-15PY-GEN-, seed-derived IDs, deterministic, validated, content-hash deduped. Ceiling 1,000,000,000 scales per file, generated on demand by the file AI — not stored files.

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Command-line interface'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Configuration JAH-15PY-000038 · compounded

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Configuration is the generator's fixed settings — prefix, hard stop, throttle, topic table — kept in the program itself, so a run needs no external config file to be reproducible.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Configuration is the generator's fixed settings — prefix, hard stop, throttle, topic table — kept in the program itself, so a run needs no external config file to be reproducible.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator configuration — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Keep GEN_PREFIX, HARD_STOP, THROTTLE_PER_CALL, and TOPICS as module constants; a run with the same constants and seed reproduces the same output. Settings are never read from the environment.

The Answer

Configuration-as-constants is the template's design and is proven by the self-test's reproducibility.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Configuration'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7

        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)

                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

  • JAH Network AI Access guide (docs/AI_ACCESS.md) — The documented read paths (registry, mirrors, record API, MCP) each capability is measured against.
  • JAH Network Master List (ChatGPT catalog, 2026-11-12) — The catalog requirement each system capability is specified from; catalog text is held package-only in the master-list program directory.

Logging JAH-15PY-000039 · complex

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Logging records what the generator did — seeds run, records made, failures — to stderr, so a run can be audited without polluting the records on stdout.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Logging records what the generator did — seeds run, records made, failures — to stderr, so a run can be audited without polluting the records on stdout.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator logging — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Print records to stdout (one JSON object per line) and all diagnostics to stderr; a run's log states its seed range and its record count. Logs are never mixed into record output.

The Answer

Logging discipline is implemented in the template CLI; pilot demos print only the generated record and the digests.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Logging'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Self-tests JAH-15PY-000040 · simple

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Self-tests prove the generator works before it is trusted: determinism, validation, seed sensitivity, score range, hash stability, and throttle — eight checks, all passing.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

The Problem

The Worked Solution

Run run_tests() before accepting any generator; a single failing check blocks the generator. The test list is fixed in the template and inherited by every pilot.

The Answer

Self-tests are proven: template 8/8 PASS, and every pilot demo re-runs the equivalent checks (determinism + validation) on its own records.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Self-tests'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Sources

  • JAH Network live manifest (63,047 databases / 12,349,330 records) — Network scale: database and record counts used in every key_figures set.
  • JAH Network AI Access guide (docs/AI_ACCESS.md) — The documented read paths (registry, mirrors, record API, MCP) each capability is measured against.
  • JAH Network Master List (ChatGPT catalog, 2026-11-12) — The catalog requirement each system capability is specified from; catalog text is held package-only in the master-list program directory.

Benchmarking JAH-15PY-000041 · compounded

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Benchmarking measures how fast the generator runs — records per second on the reference machine — so slow generators can be found and slow calls avoided.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Benchmarking measures how fast the generator runs — records per second on the reference machine — so slow generators can be found and slow calls avoided.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator benchmarking — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Time a standard batch (100 records) and report the elapsed time with the machine's description. A benchmark without its machine description cannot be compared with anything.

The Answer

Benchmarking is a design in this record: pilot generators run in milliseconds per record, and no formal benchmark was measured — the record does not invent one.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 0, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Benchmarking'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Security JAH-15PY-000042 · complex

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Security means the generator never executes its inputs: seeds are integers, topics come from a fixed table, and no input text is ever evaluated as code.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Security means the generator never executes its inputs: seeds are integers, topics come from a fixed table, and no input text is ever evaluated as code.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator security — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Validate the seed as an integer and the topic against the fixed table before generating; reject anything else. The generator imports only the standard library and touches no network or filesystem path from its inputs.

The Answer

Security is by construction (stdlib-only, no eval, no network); no hostile-input test suite was run, and the record says so.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

Run 2 SHA-256: 7117bc319c5688ea33b5a63f43cf134df578a31b82a544290a51f50b4e4f67c1

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Security'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

  • JAH Network live manifest (63,047 databases / 12,349,330 records) — Network scale: database and record counts used in every key_figures set.
  • JAH Network AI Access guide (docs/AI_ACCESS.md) — The documented read paths (registry, mirrors, record API, MCP) each capability is measured against.
  • JAH Network Master List (ChatGPT catalog, 2026-11-12) — The catalog requirement each system capability is specified from; catalog text is held package-only in the master-list program directory.

Documentation JAH-15PY-000043 · simple

Evidence: established Public record: the Python generator described here was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2); its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte. Facts about the companion database are verified against the live database it companions.

Documentation is generated from the generator's actual code — its contract, its CLI, its tests — in PATTERN.md and the template, so the docs can never drift from what the generator does.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

Description

Documentation is generated from the generator's actual code — its contract, its CLI, its tests — in PATTERN.md and the template, so the docs can never drift from what the generator does.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for 15-Minute-Cities Python Generator documentation — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Write the pattern from the template's real behaviour (verified by running it), and re-verify the docs whenever the template changes. A doc claim that contradicts a test run is removed, not kept.

The Answer

PATTERN.md was written from the executed template (8/8 self-tests, CLI runs shown); the docs match the code.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Documentation'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Provenance JAH-15PY-000044 · compounded

Provenance records where a generated record came from — which generator, which seed, which pattern version — inside the record itself, so its authority can be judged.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database 15-minute-cities.

The Worked Solution

The Answer

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 288, 'covered': 288, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Provenance'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-15PY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

  • JAH Network live manifest (63,047 databases / 12,349,330 records) — Network scale: database and record counts used in every key_figures set.
  • JAH Network AI Access guide (docs/AI_ACCESS.md) — The documented read paths (registry, mirrors, record API, MCP) each capability is measured against.
  • JAH Network Master List (ChatGPT catalog, 2026-11-12) — The catalog requirement each system capability is specified from; catalog text is held package-only in the master-list program directory.
